- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gustavus Adolphus, leader of Sweden (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Sweden
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hakkapeliitta, Replacing Cuirassier (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Carolean, Replacing Fusilier (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Nobel Committee, Replacing Foreign Bureau (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: Bastu, Replacing Bath (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Civilization Ability: Melee Land Units start with the Gå-På Promotion, and Siege Units start with the Mobility Promotion. All Military Units heal 10 HP and earn 2 XP when a Great Person is born. +15% Combat Strength from / Leadership.
      - Leader: Gustavus Adolphus
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      - Nuclear Fusion: Allows you to build the SS Booster, a component necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Future Tech
      
      ## Policies
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: Turn 441: Maximum aggression maintained. War with Songhai unavoidable in 7 turns when peace treaty expires. Songhai threatens immediate diplomatic victory 2 turns later. No diplomatic maneuvering possible - only total war to capture three capitals. Chattiness at 3 to avoid distractions.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Nuclear Fusion
        - Rationale: Turn 456: After Telecommunications completes (9 turns), queue Nuclear Fusion to unlock SS Booster. While Domination is the primary goal requiring capture of three capitals, Science Victory provides a critical backup path if Songhai's defenses delay the conquest. Having Apollo Program completed since Turn 456 allows pivot to spaceship parts if necessary.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Air Supremacy (Policy)
        - Rationale: Turn 456: Confirming Air Supremacy (Level 3 Autocracy tenet) as the priority policy selection when culture threshold reached in 11 turns. Free Airports in every city and +25% aircraft production will provide essential infrastructure and production bonuses to sustain the final offensive against Songhai's capitals.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: The Iroquois have been defeated as a major civilization with the capture of Onondaga. Resetting relationship modifiers to 0/0 reflects the end of hostilities. Their empire is now incorporated into Sweden, making diplomatic calculations with them irrelevant. This clears the diplomatic slate to focus on the remaining active competitors: Ethiopia (primary Spaceship rival) and Rome (ongoing war).
        - UpdatedTurn: 224
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Rome has been completely defeated with the capture of their capital. Resetting diplomatic modifiers to neutral (0/0) reflects the end of hostilities and allows the AI to focus diplomatic calculations on active threats, particularly Ethiopia which is the primary Spaceship competitor.
        - UpdatedTurn: 258
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Turn 346: Carthage effectively defeated - only 4 weak cities remain with 684 military strength vs my 7240. Original capital captured. Resetting relationship to 0/0 to clear diplomatic hostility modifiers and free AI focus for imminent Spain war in 3 turns. Will finish cleanup after Spain eliminated.
        - UpdatedTurn: 346
        
        ### Ethiopia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Turn 422: Ethiopia effectively neutralized with capture of Addis Ababa. They retain only Awasa (population 17, 342 military strength, 100% war weariness) and pose no strategic threat. Resetting relationship from war hostility to neutral (0/0) clears diplomatic bandwidth to focus entirely on Songhai, the true remaining adversary holding the three capitals we need. Allows AI to redirect diplomatic calculations toward the primary target.
        - UpdatedTurn: 422
        
        ### Spain
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Turn 451: Spain is no longer useful as a diplomatic distraction since Decolonization already passed. Resetting relationship to neutral (0/0) to clear diplomatic bandwidth and focus entirely on the war against Songhai. Spain is Songhai's vassal with Madrid (a capital I need), so they may become collateral damage in the coming offensive.
        - UpdatedTurn: 451
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Turn 456: Songhai holds Paris, Gao, and Madrid - the three capitals needed for Domination Victory. With diplomatic victory imminent in 5 turns (41 delegates vs 39 needed), maximum hostility maintained. Total war until all three capitals are captured - no peace, no negotiation, only unconditional surrender through conquest.
        - UpdatedTurn: 456
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Turn 457: Critical 4-turn countdown to capture Paris, Gao, and Madrid before Songhai wins Diplomatic Victory. Current strategy maximized for rapid conquest: Stealth Bombers and XCOM units in production, Air Supremacy policy queued, maximum offensive flavors active. Overwhelming 5:1 military advantage (210k vs 40k). Peace treaty expired - total war offensive must proceed immediately. No strategic adjustments needed - configuration is optimized for final Domination push.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 39
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 3 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Songhai Sphere of Influence: Wanggeom:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Songhai Sphere of Influence: Vancouver:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 428
          - World Religion: Catholicism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.

        ### 93
        - Type: PlayerDoneTurn
        - NextPlayer: 3: Songhai
        - Player: 2: Sweden
        
        You, Gustavus Adolphus (leader of Sweden, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 458.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gustavus Adolphus, leader of Sweden (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Sweden
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hakkapeliitta, Replacing Cuirassier (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Carolean, Replacing Fusilier (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Nobel Committee, Replacing Foreign Bureau (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: Bastu, Replacing Bath (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Civilization Ability: Melee Land Units start with the Gå-På Promotion, and Siege Units start with the Mobility Promotion. All Military Units heal 10 HP and earn 2 XP when a Great Person is born. +15% Combat Strength from / Leadership.
      - Leader: Gustavus Adolphus
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      - Nuclear Fusion: Allows you to build the SS Booster, a component necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Future Tech
      
      ## Policies
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Victory Conditions**
            
            My current focus is on assessing our progress toward a domination victory. I'm reviewing the capitals we control, noting our ownership of Onondaga and Stockholm, alongside Rome. This analysis forms the basis for our next strategic moves.
            
            **Analyzing Victory Threats**
            
            I am currently analyzing potential threats to our victory. Songhai's significant delegate count poses a diplomatic threat, nearing a victory in the World Congress. I'm also reviewing their military control of key capitals, assessing their domination victory prospects.
            
            **Declaring War Against Songhai**
            
            My thoughts are now centered on the imminent declaration of war against Songhai. The expired peace treaty and our overwhelming military superiority of over 5:1 confirm this is the correct course. While we are "Planning War," the staff will execute the tactical declaration soon.
            
            **Initiating Hostilities**
            
            I'm confirming the ongoing preparations for war with Songhai. While I've adjusted our strategic flavors to maximum offense and confirmed our hostile relationship, I'm double-checking that the staff is interpreting this as an immediate directive for war, and not just an intention. I will also quickly scan for any emergent Science Victory threats before fully committing.
            
            **Conquering Capitals**
            
            My focus has shifted to the immediate capture of Songhai's capitals. With Sweden nearing a science victory and Songhai threatening diplomacy, we must secure Paris, Gao, and Madrid swiftly. My units are positioned to launch assaults on these key strongholds.
            
            **Preparing Invasion**
            
            My thoughts are now focused on the advantageous geographic positioning for an invasion of Songhai. We possess a vastly superior military, equipped with advanced units, making an assault on their capitals the most logical next step in our domination victory pursuit.
            
            **Revising Technology Path**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our technology choices. Given our imminent victory drive and potential threats, focusing on military conquest is paramount. I'm assessing whether to maintain Nuclear Fusion for its science backup or if an alternative technology better supports our rapid domination goal, considering the endgame turn count.
            
            **Selecting New Policies**
            
            I am evaluating our next policy selection. Considering our strong offensive posture, "Air Supremacy" appears highly beneficial for rapid unit deployment and production bonuses, especially with its synergy for airlift capabilities. I'm weighing this against other ideological advancements for our continued domination push.
            
            **Adopting Air Supremacy**
            
            My current thoughts are fixated on securing "Air Supremacy" as our next policy. The immediate benefits of free airports across all 28 cities for production and airlift capabilities are immense and far outweigh other options for our immediate domination push. I'm confirming this selection.
            
            **Implementing Air Supremacy**
            
            I am now focused on implementing "Air Supremacy" via `set-policy`. I'm also confirming our relationships, noting Carthage as a friendly vassal and Spain as a neighbor planning war and a vassal of Songhai. My current understanding is that Madrid is a Songhai-controlled capital, not Spain's original.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Victory**
            
            I'm now reassessing our path to victory, realizing the World Congress vote on Global Hegemony is imminent. With only three turns remaining, I'm analyzing if our current military operations can secure a domination victory, or if a pivot towards diplomatic victory is necessary to win before the vote.
            
            **Assessing Unit Deployment**
            
            I'm currently reviewing our unit locations and strengths in various zones, noting concentrations like those in Zone 14700 (Shanghai) and Zone 15299 (Addis Ababa). This assessment is critical for understanding our immediate offensive capabilities against Songhai's capitals.
            
            **Deploying Western Forces**
            
            I'm reassessing our western army's strength, realizing its considerable power. With roughly four stealth bombers, one heavy bomber, modern armor, and multiple mechanized infantry, the question remains whether this force is sufficient to capture Songhai's key capitals within the tight three-turn window for victory.
            
            **Evaluating XCOM Capabilities**
            
            My thoughts are now on leveraging XCOM squads for rapid deployment. I've identified that they can paradrop up to 40 tiles, and we possess two. However, our severe Gold Per Turn deficit is a critical concern, prompting an investigation into the cause.
            
            **Addressing Gold Deficit**
            
            I'm urgently addressing our critical -1182 Gold Per Turn deficit. New caravans are being deployed to establish trade routes, and I'm increasing our Gold flavor to prioritize wealth-generating buildings to prevent bankruptcy and its severe penalties.
            
            **Shifting Diplomatic Focus**
            
            I'm now intensely focused on disrupting Songhai's diplomatic victory by monitoring city-state alliances, especially Hanoi's recent shift to Sweden's favor, which significantly impacts their vote count. This development requires a rapid re-evaluation of our diplomatic strategies to counter Songhai's remaining influence.
            
            **Disrupting Diplomatic Influence**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our strategy to counter Songhai's diplomatic lead. Discovering numerous defeated city-states has opened a new avenue: conquering their remaining allied city-states, specifically Vancouver and Wanggeom, to deny Songhai their delegate votes. This presents a difficult trade-off, as it diverts military resources from our primary objective of capturing Songhai's capitals within the crucial two-turn window.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Threshold**
            
            I'm currently analyzing the World Congress delegate count. Songhai currently holds 41 votes, exceeding the 39 required for a diplomatic victory. I'm evaluating options to reduce their delegate count by attacking their allied city-states, Wanggeom and Vancouver, though their high defenses present a challenge.
            
            **Disrupting Delegate Count**
            
            I'm assessing how to diminish Songhai's delegate advantage. Capturing their cities, particularly capitals like Gao, Paris, and Madrid, is key for a domination victory. I'm also investigating if their delegate count can be directly reduced by taking these specific locations, or if I need to target allied city-states instead.
            
            **Conquering Allied City-States**
            
            I'm focusing on how conquering Songhai's allied city-states, Wanggeom and Vancouver, can directly reduce their delegate count. This might be the fastest way to deny them a diplomatic victory within the two-turn window, despite the high defensive capabilities of these targets.
            
            **Assessing XCOM Reachability**
            
            My current thoughts revolve around our XCOM squads' ability to reach key city-states. While one squad is too distant, another can reach Vancouver within its 40-tile paradrop range, provided I can afford to buy them using faith, given our severe gold deficit.
            
            **Prioritizing Production**
            
            I'm assessing our production capabilities to churn out the necessary units. Cumae and Ravenna's high production, coupled with upcoming stealth bombers, is promising. I'm investigating faith purchase options for units, given our severe gold deficit, to accelerate our military buildup for victory.
            
            **Rebasing Stealth Bombers**
            
            My focus is now on rapidly deploying stealth bombers to Lhasa. This positioning is critical to strike Gao swiftly, given the close proximity. I'm confirming that our high production cities can build needed units and that our flavors are set for maximum offense.
            
            **Bombarding Key Capitals**
            
            I'm confirming our stealth bombers can reach all three Songhai capitals from our advantageous city positions. Lhasa, Uppsala, Mek'ele, and Bahir Dar create a powerful offensive arc, enabling immediate strikes on Gao, Paris, and Madrid upon declaring war. This strategic positioning is incredibly promising.
            
            **Assessing Air Superiority**
            
            I'm confirming our massive bomber force of 25 stealth and 17 heavy bombers. This overwhelming air power will allow us to obliterate Songhai's capitals in a single turn, securing our domination victory.
            
            **Capturing Gao**
            
            I'm focusing on the immediate capture of Gao. My bombers are poised to reduce its defenses, allowing an XCOM squad to paradrop in and secure it, a critical step for our domination victory.
            
            **Confirming Flavor Settings**
            
            I'm reviewing our current flavor settings to ensure they align with our offensive strategy. With offense, air, and mobilization all at maximum, and gold at a healthy 90, our focus is clearly on rapid conquest. I'm considering whether to explicitly confirm these settings or maintain the status quo.
            
            **Adjusting Flavor Settings**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our current flavor settings, specifically Gold and Diplomacy. With a significant gold deficit, increasing Gold flavor seems prudent. However, given our diplomatic situation and the inability to buy city-states, increasing Diplomacy flavor might not offer immediate benefits for our current victory push.
            
            **Optimizing Flavor and Persona**
            
            I've confirmed our flavor settings are highly optimized for our current domination push, with offense, air, and mobilization maxed. Resources indicate we can build nukes if we capture Paris, but our bomber superiority makes them unnecessary. I've also analyzed our Persona, finding it well-suited for competitive play with minimal wonder focus, aligning perfectly with our rapid victory objective.
            
            **Setting Hostile Relationships**
            
            I'm adjusting our relationships to ensure maximum aggression. I've set Spain to a hostile stance to prompt immediate attacks on their border cities, which will aid in clearing Songhai's flanks. This aligns with our current persona and overall strategy.
            
            **Coordinating Joint Offensives**
            
            I'm focusing on setting Spain to a hostile -100/-100 relationship. This is crucial to coordinate a joint offensive, treating them as a co-belligerent alongside Songhai and ensuring our military attacks are synchronized. I am also confirming "Nuclear Fusion" as the next technology and "Air Supremacy" as the next policy.
            
            **Setting Hostile Relationships**
            
            I am setting Spain to a hostile stance to coordinate a joint offensive. This ensures our military actions are synchronized, treating them as a co-belligerent alongside Songhai. I am also confirming "Nuclear Fusion" as our next technology and "Air Supremacy" as our next policy.
            
            **Coordinating Joint Offensives**
            
            I am setting Spain to a hostile stance to coordinate a joint offensive. This ensures our military actions are synchronized, treating them as a co-belligerent alongside Songhai. I am also confirming "Nuclear Fusion" as our next technology and "Air Supremacy" as our next policy.
            
            **Researching Nuclear Fusion**
            
            I am confirming Nuclear Fusion as our next technology. While our immediate goal is conquering Songhai, continuing our science path keeps our late-game options open and maintains high scores. I am also implementing Air Supremacy policy for enhanced airlift capabilities.
            
            **Adjusting XCOM Use**
            
            My focus is on utilizing XCOM squads effectively. I'm prioritizing faith purchases for units due to the gold deficit and assessing if their 40-tile paradrop can reach Vancouver. My bombers are now positioned for swift strikes on Songhai's capitals, enabling a rapid domination victory.
